- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `internal/modules/npm/instance_upgrade.go`**

Constants the spec names, declared in this file:

```go
const (
	// instanceUpgradeAPIWait is how long the new container may take to answer before the upgrade is rolled back.
	instanceUpgradeAPIWait = 120 * time.Second
	// instanceUpgradePoll is the wait between two looks at the new container's API.
	instanceUpgradePoll = time.Second
	// instanceUpgradeKeep is the number of archive sets that pruning keeps.
	instanceUpgradeKeep = 3
)
```

*Declaration.* `func (n nm) instanceUpgrade() core.ActionDef` is `core.DefineMutating[UpgradeInput, Upgraded]` with `Name: ID + ".instance.upgrade"`, `Version: 1`, `Class: core.ClassCompensable`, `Side: core.SideAgent`, `LockKeys: []string{lockKey}`, `ApplyTimeout: 20 * time.Minute`, `LongRunning: true`, no guard and no `Available`, and the handler `instanceUpgradeHandler{n: n}`. The target is always the image pinned in this release, `Image`; an instance that already runs it is upgraded too (its container and volumes are recreated), which the harness relies on. The action's one effect has the resource `resourceOf(nounInstance, "image")` (`npm.instance:image`).

*Precheck, shared by `Plan` and `Apply`.* Load the instance (`loadInstance(ctx, env.DB.Read())`): none is `core.Definite(errcode.New(errcode.NotFound, ...))`. Inspect the container the row names (`InspectContainer` by id): not found is `core.Definite(errcode.New(errcode.NPMUnhealthy, ...))` (the instance's container is missing); another Engine error is `core.Definite(errcode.Wrap(errcode.DockerEngineUnavailable, ...))`. Return the row and the container.

*`Plan`.* After the precheck, the diff is exactly these four lines: `-` `image <row.Image>`, `+` `image <Image>`, ` ` `both volumes (<dataVolume>, <certVolume>) are archived to <backupDir> first`, ` ` `the original container and volumes are restored if the new container's API does not answer within 120 s`. `StateHash` is the hex SHA-256 of the container id, `row.Image` and `Image`. `Plan` changes nothing on the host and journals nothing.

*Configuration.* `instanceUpgradeConfig(c dockerapi.ContainerJSON) containerConfig` builds what is journaled to restore the original: `Name` is the container's name without its leading `/` (`containerName` when empty); `Create` is a `dockerapi.ContainerCreate` with `Name`, the container's `ContainerConfig` with `Hostname` cleared, `HostConfig` a pointer to a copy of the container's, and `NetworkingConfig` holding one endpoint for `networkName` that carries only the container's `IPAMConfig` for that network (an empty endpoint when the container reports none); `Networks` holds, for every other network the container is on, an endpoint with only its `IPAMConfig` (the runtime fields `NetworkID`, `EndpointID`, addresses, `MacAddress`, `DNSNames` and `Aliases` are left out, since Docker assigns them again).

*`Apply`, in this order.*
1. The precheck.
2. Make the target image available: `InspectImage(Image)`; when that fails for any reason, `PullImage(Image)`, read the stream with `Next` until `io.EOF` (a message whose `Err()` is set, or a stream error, fails the pull), close it, and inspect again. A failure here is returned as `core.Definite(fmt.Errorf("npm: pull %s: %w", ...))`: nothing has changed yet.
3. Build the configuration from the container, then journal the effect: `core.Effect{Kind: core.EffectModify, Resource: resourceOf(nounInstance, "image"), Local: true, Before: <image document of row.Image>, After: <image document of Image>}` through `env.Effects.Intend(ctx, e, nil)`, with `InverseLocal` left false (the inverse drives the Engine, which is not local, spec 1.2). The image document is `core.StateDoc{State: {"image": "<ref>"}}`.
4. Insert the transaction row, phase `recorded`: `insertUpgrade` with `FromImage` `row.Image`, `ToImage` `Image`, the configuration and no archives. If the insert fails, `env.Effects.Skipped` the effect and return the error: nothing has changed.
5. The steps below, each writing its phase with `setUpgradePhase(ctx, env.DB, env.ChangeID, phase, "", archives, env.Clock.Now())` before the host change it covers (the archives argument is always the full list known at that point):
   - phase `stopped`: `StopContainer(row.ContainerID, 30 s)`.
   - phase `archiving` (the archives list now names both files, with empty checksums): for the data volume then the certificate volume, read `CopyFromContainer(row.ContainerID, "/data")` and `"/etc/letsencrypt"` to the end, write the bytes with `env.Files.WriteManaged(ctx, ID, backupDir, <archiveName for env.ChangeID and the volume>, data, 0o600, 0, 0)`, read the file back through `deps.readFile(backupDir + "/" + name)` and fail unless the SHA-256 of what was read equals the SHA-256 of what was written. The names are `<lowercased change id>-data.tar` and `<lowercased change id>-letsencrypt.tar`; read the doc comment of `archiveName` (Task 11) for the form of its second argument. Then phase `archived` with the checksums filled in.
   - phase `recreating`: `RemoveContainer(row.ContainerID)` without volumes; `CreateContainer(instanceCreate(Image))`, exactly that configuration; `ConnectNetwork(network, newID, endpoint)` for every entry of the journaled `Networks`, in name order; `StartContainer(newID)`.
   - phase `waiting`: wait for the new container's API, below.
   - finish: update the instance row (`ContainerID` the new id, `Image` `Image`, `UpdatedAt` now, everything else as loaded, so the public mark stays) with `saveInstance` inside `env.DB.Write`; phase `done`; `env.Effects.Made(ctx, seq, resource, <image document of Image>)`; then prune archives (below). The output is `Upgraded{FromImage: row.Image, ToImage: Image}`.
6. A failing step runs `rollback` under `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` on the transaction row loaded afresh, and then returns the failure. A step failure after the effect was journaled is never `core.Definite`. When the failing step is the API wait the error is `errcode.Wrap(errcode.NPMUnhealthy, cause, ...)` whose detail says the new instance did not answer within 120 s and whether the original was restored; any other failure is `fmt.Errorf("npm: upgrade step %s failed: %w ...")` saying the same. When `rollback` itself fails, the returned error says the rollback failed and the instance needs repair.

*Waiting for the API.* A probe is ready when `deps.anon().Health` reports `Setup` true and `deps.api(ctx, env)` yields a client whose `ProxyHosts` call succeeds. The first probe is immediate; then one probe every `instanceUpgradePoll` on `env.Clock` (`AfterFunc`, as derp's `await` does) until a probe is ready or `instanceUpgradeAPIWait` has passed since the first, when the last probe's error is returned; a cancelled `ctx` ends the wait. Each probe is bounded by the time left, and by at least one poll.

*Pruning.* After phase `done`, list `deps.readDir(backupDir)` (a missing directory is an empty list). An archive set is the files of one change: the name up to its last `-`, for the names that end in `.tar`. Keep the `instanceUpgradeKeep` newest sets (a larger name sorts newer: change ids are ULIDs) and every set that a transaction in `npm_upgrades` whose phase is neither `done` nor `rolled_back` refers to through `archives[].file` (a transaction in phase `failed` still needs its archives); remove the files of every other set with `env.Files.RemoveManaged(ctx, ID, backupDir, name)`. A pruning failure is logged on `env.Log` when it is set and does not fail the upgrade.

*`rollback(ctx, env, u)`.* It works from the transaction row alone and is safe to run again, so it records no phase of its own while it runs.
- Phase `rolled_back`: return nil.
- Phases `recorded`, `stopped`, `archiving`, `archived`: resume. In `archiving`, remove every file the row's archives list names, ignoring `fs.ErrNotExist` (the list holds both names from the start of the phase). Then `StartContainer` the instance row's container (a container that runs already is fine).
- Phases `recreating`, `waiting`, `done` (undo of a finished upgrade) and `failed`: restore. First verify that the row holds two archives with checksums and that each file read through `deps.readFile` has its checksum; otherwise fail before touching anything. Then remove the container named `containerName` (force), remove both volumes (force; not-found is fine) and create them again labelled `core.LabelModule: ID`, create the original container from `u.Config.Create` with `u.Config.Name`, connect the `u.Config.Networks`, `CopyToContainer` the data archive to `/` and the certificate archive to `/etc`, start it, and save the instance row with the new container id and `u.FromImage`.
- Success ends with phase `rolled_back` (archives kept in the row) and the change's `intended` effect of `npm.instance:image`, if any, `Skipped`.
- Any error ends with phase `failed`, `result` the first line of the error cut to 200 characters, and the error returned.

*`Observe(ctx, env, e)`.* Only the resource `npm.instance:image` is accepted. It inspects the container named `containerName`: not found gives `nullState()`; otherwise `core.StateDoc{State: {"image": <Config.Image>}}`; always `Matches: 1`, `Exclusive: true`.

*`Verify(ctx, env, in, out)`.* It reads the host only and works with an empty `out`: the instance row's image is `Image`, the container named `containerName` is the one the row names, runs and was created from `Image`, and a probe as above is ready (no waiting).

No comment names a task or a plan. Error strings start with `npm:`; no detail, log line or error carries the admin password.

package npm

	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/coretest"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/errcode"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/schema"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/modules/firewall"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/modules/firewall/dockerfw"
)

const ckHost = "h7"

// ckAgent is what one host's agent answers to the controller: the firewall.docker resource and the
// npm.instance.status read. It records what was asked.
type ckAgent struct {
	docker    firewall.DockerStatus
	dockerErr error
	status    InstanceStatus
	statusErr error

	mu        sync.Mutex
	reads     []string // "<action>/v<version> <input>"
	resources []string
}

func (a *ckAgent) fake() *coretest.FakeAgent {
	return &coretest.FakeAgent{
		ReadFunc: func(_ context.Context, _ core.Principal, ref core.ActionRef, input json.RawMessage) (json.RawMessage, error) {
			a.mu.Lock()
			a.reads = append(a.reads, ref.String()+" "+string(input))
			a.mu.Unlock()
			if ref.Name != "npm.instance.status" || ref.Version != 1 {
				return nil, fmt.Errorf("ckAgent: no read %s", ref)
			}
			if a.statusErr != nil {
				return nil, a.statusErr
			}
			return json.Marshal(a.status)
		},
		ResourceFunc: func(_ context.Context, _ core.Principal, name string) (json.RawMessage, error) {
			a.mu.Lock()
			a.resources = append(a.resources, name)
			a.mu.Unlock()
			if name != "firewall.docker" {
				return nil, fmt.Errorf("ckAgent: no resource %s", name)
			}
			if a.dockerErr != nil {
				return nil, a.dockerErr
			}
			return json.Marshal(a.docker)
		},
	}
}

// ckWorld is a controller whose only host, ckHost, is served by a.
func ckWorld(t *testing.T, a *ckAgent) *thHost {
	t.Helper()
	h := newTHHost(t)
	h.agents = map[string]*coretest.FakeAgent{ckHost: a.fake()}
	return h
}

// ckDockerOK is a supported host with the integration on: Docker 29.9.1 with raw protection.
func ckDockerOK() firewall.DockerStatus {
	return firewall.DockerStatus{
		Available: true, Version: "29.9.1", Driver: "iptables", RawProtection: true, Supported: true, Enabled: true, Health: "ok",
	}
}

// ckBinding is a stored binding of selector with the sources allowed.
func ckBinding(selector string, port uint16, proto string, allowed ...string) firewall.DockerBinding {
	b := dockerfw.Binding{Selector: selector, HostPort: port, Proto: proto}
	return firewall.DockerBinding{Binding: b, ID: b.ID(), Containers: []string{}, Allowed: allowed, Denied: []string{}}
}

// ckPorts is the two bindings npm.instance.deploy-public's ports step stores.
func ckPorts() []firewall.DockerBinding {
	return []firewall.DockerBinding{
		ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 80, "tcp", "any"),
		ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 443, "tcp", "any"),
	}
}

// ckRun reads def through the handler the engine uses for an internal read and decodes its answer.
func ckRun[Out any](t *testing.T, def core.ActionDef, env core.Env, in any) (Out, error) {
	t.Helper()
	raw, err := json.Marshal(in)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if def.Handler.Reveal == nil {
		t.Fatalf("%s has no Reveal handler", def.Spec.Name)
	}
	_, out, err := def.Handler.Reveal(context.Background(), env, raw)
	var got Out
	if err != nil {
		return got, err
	}
	if err := json.Unmarshal(out, &got); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("output %s: %v", out, err)
	}
	return got, nil
}

func ckCode(err error) errcode.Code {
	var coded *errcode.Error
	if errors.As(err, &coded) {
		return coded.Code
	}
	return ""
}

func ckDetail(err error) string {
	var coded *errcode.Error
	if errors.As(err, &coded) {
		return coded.Detail
	}
	return ""
}

// TestCkDeclarations: both checks are internal controller reads without locks and have a schema.
func TestCkDeclarations(t *testing.T) {
	n := newTHHost(t).nm()
	tests := []struct {
		name    string
		def     core.ActionDef
		in, out reflect.Type
	}{
		{"npm.instance.check", n.instanceCheck(), reflect.TypeFor[CheckInput](), reflect.TypeFor[CheckResult]()},
		{"npm.public.check", n.publicCheck(), reflect.TypeFor[PublicInput](), reflect.TypeFor[PublicResult]()},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		s := tc.def.Spec
		if s.Name != tc.name || s.Version != 1 || s.Summary == "" || s.Class != core.ClassRead || s.Side != core.SideController ||
			!s.Internal || s.Reveal || len(s.LockKeys) != 0 || tc.def.Handler.Reveal == nil || tc.def.Handler.Read != nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: spec %+v, reveal handler %v, read handler %v; want an internal controller read v1 without locks, served by Reveal only",
				tc.name, s, tc.def.Handler.Reveal != nil, tc.def.Handler.Read != nil)
		}
		if tc.def.InputType != tc.in || tc.def.OutputType != tc.out {
			t.Errorf("%s: types %v and %v, want %v and %v", tc.name, tc.def.InputType, tc.def.OutputType, tc.in, tc.out)
		}
		if _, err := schema.ActionSnapshot(tc.def); err != nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: schema: %v", tc.name, err)
		}
	}
}

// TestCkInstanceCheck: the check refuses a host that cannot run NPM before it compares the integration, and
// refuses an integration that is not as planned with plan.state_changed.
func TestCkInstanceCheck(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name        string
		docker      func(d *firewall.DockerStatus)
		integration bool
		want        errcode.Code // "" for an answer
		detail      string       // a substring of the refusal's detail
	}{
		{"the integration is on and supported, as planned", nil, true, "", ""},
		{"the integration is off, as planned", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) { d.Enabled = false }, false, "", ""},
		{"the integration is on but unsupported, which counts as off", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) {
			d.Supported, d.Unsupported = false, []string{"UFW's iptables variant is not known"}
		}, false, "", ""},
		{"Docker 28.0 is enough", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) { d.Version = "28.0.0" }, true, "", ""},
		{"the integration was turned on since the plan", nil, false, errcode.PlanStateChanged, "integration"},
		{"the integration was turned off since the plan", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) { d.Enabled = false }, true, errcode.PlanStateChanged, "integration"},
		{"the host stopped supporting the integration since the plan", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) { d.Supported = false }, true, errcode.PlanStateChanged, "integration"},
		{"Docker is older than 28.0", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) { d.Version, d.Supported = "27.5.1", false }, false, errcode.ModuleUnavailable, "27.5.1"},
		{"Docker's version is not recognised", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) { d.Version = "latest" }, false, errcode.ModuleUnavailable, "latest"},
		{"the raw-table protection is not loaded", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) { d.RawProtection, d.Supported = false, false }, false, errcode.ModuleUnavailable, "raw"},
		{"Docker does not answer", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) {
			*d = firewall.DockerStatus{Available: false, Reason: "the Docker socket is missing"}
		}, false, errcode.ModuleUnavailable, "the Docker socket is missing"},
		{"an unusable host is reported before a changed integration", func(d *firewall.DockerStatus) { d.Version, d.Supported = "27.5.1", false }, true, errcode.ModuleUnavailable, "28.0"},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			a := &ckAgent{docker: ckDockerOK()}
			if tc.docker != nil {
				tc.docker(&a.docker)
			}
			h := ckWorld(t, a)
			got, err := ckRun[CheckResult](t, h.nm().instanceCheck(), h.ctlEnv(""), CheckInput{HostID: ckHost, Integration: tc.integration})
			if tc.want == "" {
				if err != nil || got != (CheckResult{Integration: tc.integration}) {
					t.Fatalf("answer %+v, %v; want integration %v", got, err, tc.integration)
				}
			} else if ckCode(err) != tc.want || !core.IsDefinite(err) || !strings.Contains(ckDetail(err), tc.detail) {
				t.Fatalf("error %#v; want a definite %s refusal whose detail holds %q", err, tc.want, tc.detail)
			}
			if !reflect.DeepEqual(a.resources, []string{"firewall.docker"}) || len(a.reads) != 0 {
				t.Errorf("asked resources %v and reads %v, want firewall.docker once and no read", a.resources, a.reads)
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestCkInstanceCheckReadErrors: a host that cannot be read is an error of its own, not a refusal that claims
// the host is unusable or changed.
func TestCkInstanceCheckReadErrors(t *testing.T) {
	boom := errors.New("the agent does not answer")
	run := func(h *thHost) error {
		_, err := ckRun[CheckResult](t, h.nm().instanceCheck(), h.ctlEnv(""), CheckInput{HostID: ckHost})
		return err
	}
	t.Run("the resource read fails", func(t *testing.T) {
		err := run(ckWorld(t, &ckAgent{dockerErr: boom}))
		if !errors.Is(err, boom) || core.IsDefinite(err) || ckCode(err) != "" {
			t.Errorf("error %#v, want the read's own error, wrapped, uncoded and not definite", err)
		}
	})
	t.Run("the resource is not JSON", func(t *testing.T) {
		h := ckWorld(t, &ckAgent{})
		h.agents[ckHost].ResourceFunc = func(context.Context, core.Principal, string) (json.RawMessage, error) {
			return json.RawMessage(`[`), nil
		}
		if err := run(h); err == nil || core.IsDefinite(err) {
			t.Errorf("error %v, want a decode error that is not definite", err)
		}
	})
	t.Run("the host is unknown", func(t *testing.T) {
		h := ckWorld(t, &ckAgent{})
		h.agents = map[string]*coretest.FakeAgent{}
		if err := run(h); ckCode(err) != errcode.NotFound {
			t.Errorf("error %#v, want %s", err, errcode.NotFound)
		}
	})
}

// TestCkPublicCheck: an instance is public through its mark, which is tried first and needs no firewall read, or
// through stored allow bindings of both ports from every source while the integration is on.
func TestCkPublicCheck(t *testing.T) {
	deployed := func(public string) InstanceStatus {
		return InstanceStatus{Deployed: true, Running: true, APIReady: true, Public: public}
	}
	withBindings := func(b ...firewall.DockerBinding) firewall.DockerStatus {
		d := ckDockerOK()
		d.Bindings = b
		return d
	}
	hostAddr := func(b firewall.DockerBinding) firewall.DockerBinding {
		b.Binding.HostAddr = netip.MustParseAddr("203.0.113.5")
		return b
	}
	tests := []struct {
		name      string
		status    InstanceStatus
		docker    firewall.DockerStatus
		want      PublicResult
		refused   bool
		resources int // firewall.docker reads
	}{
		{"a confirmed mark", deployed("confirmed"), ckDockerOK(), PublicResult{Public: true, Via: "mark"}, false, 0},
		{"the mark wins over the ports", deployed("confirmed"), withBindings(ckPorts()...), PublicResult{Public: true, Via: "mark"}, false, 0},
		{"both ports allowed from every source", deployed("no"), withBindings(ckPorts()...), PublicResult{Public: true, Via: "ports"}, false, 1},
		{"every source among several", deployed("no"),
			withBindings(ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 80, "tcp", "10.0.0.0/8", "any"), ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 443, "tcp", "any")),
			PublicResult{Public: true, Via: "ports"}, false, 1},
		{"only port 80", deployed("no"), withBindings(ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 80, "tcp", "any")), PublicResult{}, true, 1},
		{"port 443 limited to a source range", deployed("no"),
			withBindings(ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 80, "tcp", "any"), ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 443, "tcp", "10.0.0.0/8")), PublicResult{}, true, 1},
		{"bindings of another container", deployed("no"),
			withBindings(ckBinding("web", 80, "tcp", "any"), ckBinding("web", 443, "tcp", "any")), PublicResult{}, true, 1},
		{"udp bindings", deployed("no"),
			withBindings(ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 80, "udp", "any"), ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 443, "udp", "any")), PublicResult{}, true, 1},
		{"bindings of one host address", deployed("no"),
			withBindings(hostAddr(ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 80, "tcp", "any")), hostAddr(ckBinding("darkpanel-npm", 443, "tcp", "any"))), PublicResult{}, true, 1},
		{"stored bindings while the integration is off", deployed("no"),
			func() firewall.DockerStatus { d := withBindings(ckPorts()...); d.Enabled = false; return d }(), PublicResult{}, true, 1},
		{"stored bindings on an unsupported host", deployed("no"),
			func() firewall.DockerStatus { d := withBindings(ckPorts()...); d.Supported = false; return d }(), PublicResult{}, true, 1},
		{"no mark and no bindings", deployed("no"), ckDockerOK(), PublicResult{}, true, 1},
		{"no instance", InstanceStatus{}, withBindings(ckPorts()...), PublicResult{}, true, 0},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			a := &ckAgent{status: tc.status, docker: tc.docker}
			h := ckWorld(t, a)
			got, err := ckRun[PublicResult](t, h.nm().publicCheck(), h.ctlEnv(""), PublicInput{HostID: ckHost})
			if tc.refused {
				if ckCode(err) != errcode.NPMNotPublic || !core.IsDefinite(err) {
					t.Fatalf("answer %+v, error %#v; want a definite %s refusal", got, err, errcode.NPMNotPublic)
				}
				want := "not confirmed public"
				if !tc.status.Deployed {
					want = "not deployed"
				}
				if !strings.Contains(ckDetail(err), want) || !strings.Contains(ckDetail(err), ckHost) {
					t.Errorf("detail %q, want it to say %q and name host %s", ckDetail(err), want, ckHost)
				}
			} else if err != nil || got != tc.want {
				t.Fatalf("answer %+v, %v; want %+v", got, err, tc.want)
			}
			if !reflect.DeepEqual(a.reads, []string{"npm.instance.status/v1 {}"}) {
				t.Errorf("reads %v, want npm.instance.status once with an empty input", a.reads)
			}
			if len(a.resources) != tc.resources {
				t.Errorf("firewall.docker read %d times, want %d", len(a.resources), tc.resources)
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestCkPublicCheckReadErrors: a status or firewall read that fails is an error of its own, never an answer that
// the instance is not public.
func TestCkPublicCheckReadErrors(t *testing.T) {
	boom := errors.New("the agent does not answer")
	run := func(a *ckAgent) error {
		h := ckWorld(t, a)
		_, err := ckRun[PublicResult](t, h.nm().publicCheck(), h.ctlEnv(""), PublicInput{HostID: ckHost})
		return err
	}
	unmarked := InstanceStatus{Deployed: true, Public: "no"}
	for name, a := range map[string]*ckAgent{
		"the status read fails":               {statusErr: boom},
		"the firewall read fails when needed": {status: unmarked, dockerErr: boom},
	} {
		if err := run(a); !errors.Is(err, boom) || core.IsDefinite(err) || ckCode(err) != "" {
			t.Errorf("%s: error %#v, want the read's own error, wrapped, uncoded and not definite", name, err)
		}
	}
	if err := run(&ckAgent{status: InstanceStatus{Deployed: true, Public: "confirmed"}, dockerErr: boom}); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("a confirmed mark with a failing firewall read: %v, want an answer", err)
	}
}

package npm

import (
	"context"
	"database/sql"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"reflect"
	"sync/atomic"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/coretest"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/schema"
)

// ckAddHost adds a core_hosts row to the controller database.
func ckAddHost(t *testing.T, h *thHost, id, name string) {
	t.Helper()
	err := h.ctlDB.Write(context.Background(), func(tx *sql.Tx) error {
		_, err := tx.Exec(`INSERT INTO core_hosts (id, name, is_local, created_at) VALUES (?, ?, 0, ?)`,
			id, name, h.clock.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339))
		return err
	})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
}

// ckList reads npm.instance.list as the controller's read handler does.
func ckList(ctx context.Context, h *thHost) (InstancesOutput, json.RawMessage, error) {
	raw, err := h.nm().instanceList().Handler.Read(ctx, h.ctlEnv(""), json.RawMessage(`{}`))
	if err != nil {
		return InstancesOutput{}, nil, err
	}
	var out InstancesOutput
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &out); err != nil {
		return InstancesOutput{}, raw, err
	}
	return out, raw, nil
}

// ckExposed is the status of a host with a deployed, publicly confirmed instance whose admin UI is exposed.
func ckExposed() InstanceStatus {
	return InstanceStatus{
		Deployed: true, ContainerID: "abc123", Image: "jc21/nginx-proxy-manager:2.13.5@sha256:aa", PinnedImage: "jc21/nginx-proxy-manager:2.13.5@sha256:aa",
		Running: true, APIReady: true, Public: "confirmed", AdminEmail: "ops@example.com",
		AdminExposure: &AdminExposure{Hostname: "npm.example.com", ProxyHostID: 4, CertificateID: 2}, UpgradeAvailable: true,
	}
}

func TestCkInstanceListDeclaration(t *testing.T) {
	def := newTHHost(t).nm().instanceList()
	s := def.Spec
	if s.Name != "npm.instance.list" || s.Version != 1 || s.Summary == "" || s.Class != core.ClassRead || s.Side != core.SideController ||
		s.Internal || s.Reveal || len(s.LockKeys) != 0 || def.Handler.Read == nil || def.Handler.Reveal != nil {
		t.Errorf("spec %+v, read handler %v; want a public controller read v1 without locks", s, def.Handler.Read != nil)
	}
	if def.InputType != reflect.TypeFor[InstanceListInput]() || def.OutputType != reflect.TypeFor[InstancesOutput]() {
		t.Errorf("types %v and %v, want InstanceListInput and InstancesOutput", def.InputType, def.OutputType)
	}
	if _, err := schema.ActionSnapshot(def); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("schema: %v", err)
	}
}

// TestCkInstanceList: every host is listed by id with the status its agent answers, a host whose read fails or
// that has no agent is left out, and a host without an instance is listed with Deployed false.
func TestCkInstanceList(t *testing.T) {
	boom := errors.New("the agent does not answer")
	h := newTHHost(t)
	ckAddHost(t, h, "h3", "charlie")
	ckAddHost(t, h, "h1", "alpha")
	ckAddHost(t, h, "h2", "bravo")
	ckAddHost(t, h, "h4", "delta")
	h.agents = map[string]*coretest.FakeAgent{
		"h1": (&ckAgent{status: ckExposed()}).fake(),
		"h2": (&ckAgent{statusErr: boom}).fake(),
		"h3": (&ckAgent{status: InstanceStatus{}}).fake(),
	}
	got, _, err := ckList(context.Background(), h)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	want := []HostInstance{
		{HostID: "h1", HostName: "alpha", Status: ckExposed()},
		{HostID: "h3", HostName: "charlie", Status: InstanceStatus{}},
	}
	if !reflect.DeepEqual(got.Instances, want) {
		t.Errorf("instances %+v, want %+v: by host id, without the failing host h2 and the host h4 that has no agent", got.Instances, want)
	}
}

// TestCkInstanceListWithoutHosts: no host gives an empty list, never null.
func TestCkInstanceListWithoutHosts(t *testing.T) {
	h := newTHHost(t)
	_, raw, err := ckList(context.Background(), h)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var fields map[string]json.RawMessage
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &fields); err != nil || string(fields["instances"]) != "[]" {
		t.Errorf("output %s (%v), want an instances member of []", raw, err)
	}
}

// TestCkInstanceListReadsOverlap: the agents are read concurrently, so one slow host does not delay the others
// one after another. Each read waits until all three have started.
func TestCkInstanceListReadsOverlap(t *testing.T) {
	h := newTHHost(t)
	h.agents = map[string]*coretest.FakeAgent{}
	var started atomic.Int32
	release := make(chan struct{})
	for _, id := range []string{"h1", "h2", "h3"} {
		ckAddHost(t, h, id, "host "+id)
		fa := (&ckAgent{status: ckExposed()}).fake()
		inner := fa.ReadFunc
		fa.ReadFunc = func(ctx context.Context, p core.Principal, ref core.ActionRef, input json.RawMessage) (json.RawMessage, error) {
			if started.Add(1) == 3 {
				close(release)
			}
			select {
			case <-release:
			case <-time.After(5 * time.Second):
				return nil, errors.New("the reads did not overlap")
			}
			return inner(ctx, p, ref, input)
		}
		h.agents[id] = fa
	}
	got, _, err := ckList(context.Background(), h)
	if err != nil || len(got.Instances) != 3 {
		t.Fatalf("instances %+v, %v; want all three hosts", got.Instances, err)
	}
}

// TestCkInstanceListCancelled: a read cut off by a cancelled context fails the list rather than leaving the host
// out, since an empty banner would claim that nothing is exposed.
func TestCkInstanceListCancelled(t *testing.T) {
	h := newTHHost(t)
	ckAddHost(t, h, "h1", "alpha")
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	defer cancel()
	h.agents = map[string]*coretest.FakeAgent{"h1": {
		ReadFunc: func(ctx context.Context, _ core.Principal, _ core.ActionRef, _ json.RawMessage) (json.RawMessage, error) {
			cancel()
			return nil, ctx.Err()
		},
	}}
	if _, _, err := ckList(ctx, h); !errors.Is(err, context.Canceled) {
		t.Errorf("error %v, want context.Canceled", err)
	}
}

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests to verify they fail**

Run: `mkdir -p /root/.cache/w6a-tmp/23 && export TMPDIR=/root/.cache/w6a-tmp/23 GOTOOLCHAIN=local && timeout 300 go test -count=1 -run 'TestCk' ./internal/modules/npm/`
Expected: FAIL to build, naming the first undefined identifier (`n.instanceCheck undefined`). If `InstanceListInput` is undefined, Task 11's `wire.go` lacks it and the task stops until Contracts "NPM wire shapes" names it.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`controller_checks.go`:

1. `ctlDockerStatus(ctx, env, hostID) (firewall.DockerStatus, error)`: take the host's client from `env.Agents.Agent`, read the resource `firewall.docker` with `Resource(ctx, env.Principal, "firewall.docker")` and decode it into `firewall.DockerStatus`. Every failure (unknown host, read, decode) is wrapped with `fmt.Errorf("npm: ... host %s: %w", ...)` and is neither `core.Definite` nor re-coded, so the directory's `not_found` stays reachable through `errors.As` and a transport error is never mistaken for a refusal.
2. `ctlInstanceStatus(ctx, env, hostID) (InstanceStatus, error)`: the same for `Read(ctx, env.Principal, core.ActionRef{Name: "npm.instance.status", Version: 1}, json.RawMessage("{}"))`, decoded into `InstanceStatus`, with the same wrapping.
3. `ctlDockerMajor(version string) (int, bool)`: the digits before the first dot, which must be followed by a dot; anything else (empty, `latest`) is not recognised.
4. `instanceCheck` is `core.DefineInternalRead[CheckInput, CheckResult]` with `Name: ID + ".instance.check"`, `Version: 1`, `Side: core.SideController`, no lock keys, and the summary "Check, for the controller's own use, that a host can run Nginx Proxy Manager and that its Docker firewall integration is as planned." The handler reads `ctlDockerStatus` once and refuses in this order, each refusal an `errcode.New` wrapped in `core.Definite`:
   1. `!Available`: `module.unavailable`, detail "Docker is not available on this host" followed by ": " and `Reason` when `Reason` is not empty.
   2. a version `ctlDockerMajor` does not recognise: `module.unavailable`, detail `Docker version "<v>" is not recognised; Nginx Proxy Manager needs Docker 28.0 or later`; a major below 28: `module.unavailable`, detail `Docker <v> is older than 28.0, which Nginx Proxy Manager needs`.
   3. `!RawProtection`: `module.unavailable`, detail "Docker's raw-table protection of published ports is not loaded, which Nginx Proxy Manager needs because it publishes port 81 on 127.0.0.1".
   4. `Enabled && Supported` differs from `in.Integration`: `plan.state_changed`, detail "the host's Docker firewall integration is now on (off) and was off (on) when the plan was made", with the words on and off chosen from the two values.
   Otherwise it answers a `core.Revealed[CheckResult]` whose identity is "NPM instance check for host " followed by the host id and whose value has `Integration` set to `in.Integration`.
5. `publicCheck` is `core.DefineInternalRead[PublicInput, PublicResult]` with `Name: ID + ".public.check"`, the same side and version, and a summary saying it reads whether the host's NPM instance is confirmed public. The handler reads `ctlInstanceStatus` first, then decides:
   1. `!Deployed`: `npm.not_public`, `core.Definite`, detail "Nginx Proxy Manager is not deployed on host <id>"; the firewall is not read.
   2. `Public == "confirmed"`: answer `PublicResult{Public: true, Via: "mark"}` without reading the firewall.
   3. Otherwise read `ctlDockerStatus`. The instance is public through `ports` when `Enabled && Supported` and, for both 80 and 443, some binding in `Bindings` has `Binding.Selector == containerName`, that port, `Binding.Proto == "tcp"`, no host address or the unspecified address (every host address), and `"any"` among `Allowed`. Answer `PublicResult{Public: true, Via: "ports"}`.
   4. Otherwise `npm.not_public`, `core.Definite`, detail "the Nginx Proxy Manager instance on host <id> is not confirmed public: confirm that ports 80 and 443 are reachable from outside, or open them through the firewall integration".
   The identity of the answer is "NPM public check for host " + the host id. A failing status read or firewall read is returned as the wrapped error of the helper, never as `npm.not_public`; a confirmed mark never reads the firewall, so it still answers when that read would fail.

`instance_list.go`: `instanceList` is `core.DefineRead[InstanceListInput, InstancesOutput]` with `Name: ID + ".instance.list"`, `Version: 1`, `Side: core.SideController`, no lock keys, summary "List the Nginx Proxy Manager instance status of every host." `ctlListHosts` reads `SELECT id, name FROM core_hosts ORDER BY id` through `env.DB.Read()`. The handler then reads `ctlInstanceStatus` of every host concurrently, at most 8 at a time (a buffered channel as the semaphore), each goroutine writing only its own slot of a slice indexed like the hosts, so that no lock is needed and `go test -race` stays clean. A host whose client cannot be had, whose read fails, or whose answer does not decode is left out of the list; a host without an instance is listed with the zero-valued status the agent answers. After the reads finish, a non-nil `ctx.Err()` is returned as the error, because a list thinned by a cancelled request would claim that nothing is exposed. The answer holds the surviving hosts in id order as `HostInstance{HostID, HostName, Status}` and `Instances` is an empty non-nil slice when no host survives (JSON `[]`, never `null`). Error strings start with `npm:`.

```go
package npm

import (
	"context"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/hex"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/errcode"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/platform/dockerapi"
)

const (
	urChange   = "c1"
	urResource = "npm.instance:image"
	urNewID    = "new1"
)

var (
	urFrom = "jc21/nginx-proxy-manager:2.13.4@sha256:" + strings.Repeat("a", 64)
	urTo   = "jc21/nginx-proxy-manager:2.13.5@sha256:" + strings.Repeat("b", 64)
	urData = []byte("archive of /data")
	urCert = []byte("archive of /etc/letsencrypt")
)

func urSum(b []byte) string {
	s := sha256.Sum256(b)
	return hex.EncodeToString(s[:])
}

// urFixture is a host whose NPM container runs urFrom, with the transaction rows and archives a test adds.
type urFixture struct {
	t    *testing.T
	h    *thHost
	inst thInstance
	n    nm
}

func urSetup(t *testing.T) *urFixture {
	t.Helper()
	h := newTHHost(t)
	f := &urFixture{t: t, h: h, inst: h.deployed(), n: h.nm()}
	f.setImage(f.inst.ContainerID, urFrom)
	return f
}

// handler is the upgrade's handler; it fails the test when the undo or the recovery handler is missing.
func (f *urFixture) handler() core.ErasedHandler {
	f.t.Helper()
	h := f.n.instanceUpgrade().Handler
	if h.Inverse == nil || h.Recover == nil {
		f.t.Fatalf("handler has Inverse %v and Recover %v; want both", h.Inverse != nil, h.Recover != nil)
	}
	return h
}

func (f *urFixture) setImage(id, ref string) {
	f.t.Helper()
	c, ok := f.h.docker.container(id)
	if !ok {
		f.t.Fatalf("container %s is not on the fake Docker", id)
	}
	c.Image, c.Config.Image = ref, ref
	f.h.docker.putContainer(c)
}

func (f *urFixture) setRunning(id string, running bool) {
	f.t.Helper()
	c, ok := f.h.docker.container(id)
	if !ok {
		f.t.Fatalf("container %s is not on the fake Docker", id)
	}
	c.State.Running = running
	f.h.docker.putContainer(c)
}

// replace removes the original container and, when newImage is not empty, puts the container urNewID running
// newImage under the NPM container's name, as an upgrade that reached recreating leaves the host.
func (f *urFixture) replace(newImage string) {
	f.t.Helper()
	if err := f.h.docker.RemoveContainer(context.Background(), f.inst.ContainerID, dockerapi.RemoveContainerOptions{Force: true}); err != nil {
		f.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if newImage == "" {
		return
	}
	f.h.docker.putContainer(thContainer(containerName, func(c *dockerapi.ContainerJSON) {
		c.ID, c.Image, c.Config.Image, c.State.Running = urNewID, newImage, newImage, true
	}))
}

// archives writes the two verified archives to the backup directory and returns their references.
func (f *urFixture) archives() []archiveRef {
	f.t.Helper()
	refs := []archiveRef{
		{Volume: dataVolume, File: urChange + "-data.tar", SHA256: urSum(urData)},
		{Volume: certVolume, File: urChange + "-letsencrypt.tar", SHA256: urSum(urCert)},
	}
	f.h.files[backupDir+"/"+refs[0].File] = urData
	f.h.files[backupDir+"/"+refs[1].File] = urCert
	f.h.managed[backupDir+"/"+refs[0].File] = urData
	f.h.managed[backupDir+"/"+refs[1].File] = urCert
	return refs
}

// record stores the transaction of change urChange in phase.
func (f *urFixture) record(phase, result string, archives []archiveRef) {
	f.t.Helper()
	err := insertUpgrade(context.Background(), f.h.db, upgradeRow{
		ChangeID: urChange, FromImage: urFrom, ToImage: urTo, Config: containerConfig{Create: instanceCreate(urFrom), Name: containerName},
		Archives: archives, Phase: phase, Result: result, UpdatedAt: f.h.clock.Now(),
	})
	if err != nil {
		f.t.Fatal(err)
	}
}

func (f *urFixture) phase() string {
	f.t.Helper()
	u, ok, err := loadUpgrade(context.Background(), f.h.db.Read(), urChange)
	if err != nil || !ok {
		f.t.Fatalf("upgrade %s: %v, %v", urChange, ok, err)
	}
	return u.Phase
}

func (f *urFixture) recover() (core.RecoveryOutcome, error) {
	f.t.Helper()
	return f.handler().Recover(context.Background(), f.h.env(urChange))
}

func (f *urFixture) inverse(resource string) error {
	f.t.Helper()
	_, err := f.handler().Inverse(context.Background(), f.h.env(urChange), core.Effect{ChangeID: urChange, Resource: resource})
	return err
}

// callsSince are the Docker calls made after mark, which is the call count taken earlier.
func (f *urFixture) callsSince(mark int) []string { return f.h.docker.calls()[mark:] }

func (f *urFixture) mark() int { return len(f.h.docker.calls()) }

func urCalled(calls []string, method string) bool {
	for _, c := range calls {
		if strings.Contains(c, method) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

// restored fails the test unless the NPM container is the original again: a new container running urFrom, not the
// container urNewID the upgrade created, with both volumes in place and the transaction rolled back.
func (f *urFixture) restored() {
	f.t.Helper()
	c, ok := f.h.docker.container(containerName)
	if !ok || c.Config.Image != urFrom || !c.State.Running || c.ID == urNewID {
		f.t.Errorf("container %+v (found %v), want a new running container of %s", c, ok, urFrom)
	}
	if _, ok := f.h.docker.container(urNewID); ok {
		f.t.Errorf("the upgrade's container %s is still there", urNewID)
	}
	for _, v := range []string{dataVolume, certVolume} {
		if _, ok := f.h.docker.volume(v); !ok {
			f.t.Errorf("volume %s is missing", v)
		}
	}
	if got := f.phase(); got != "rolled_back" {
		f.t.Errorf("phase %q, want rolled_back", got)
	}
}

func TestUrHandlerHasInverseAndRecover(t *testing.T) {
	urSetup(t).handler()
}

// TestUrRecoverBeforeRecreation: until the original container is removed, recovery only starts it again, drops an
// archive that was being written, and leaves a verified archive alone.
func TestUrRecoverBeforeRecreation(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name       string
		phase      string
		stopped    bool
		partial    bool // a half-written archive lies in the backup directory
		archived   bool // the transaction lists verified archives
		wantStart  bool
		wantKeeps  bool // the archives are still in the backup directory afterwards
		wantNoFile bool // no archive of the change is left
	}{
		{name: "recorded, the original still runs", phase: "recorded"},
		{name: "recorded, the original is stopped", phase: "recorded", stopped: true, wantStart: true},
		{name: "stopped", phase: "stopped", stopped: true, wantStart: true},
		{name: "archiving drops the partial archive", phase: "archiving", stopped: true, partial: true, wantStart: true, wantNoFile: true},
		{name: "archived keeps the verified archives", phase: "archived", stopped: true, archived: true, wantStart: true, wantKeeps: true},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			f := urSetup(t)
			if tc.stopped {
				f.setRunning(f.inst.ContainerID, false)
			}
			var refs []archiveRef
			if tc.partial || tc.archived {
				refs = f.archives()
			}
			if !tc.archived {
				refs = nil
			}
			f.record(tc.phase, "", refs)
			mark := f.mark()
			out, err := f.recover()
			if err != nil || out.State != core.RecoveryRolledBack {
				t.Fatalf("outcome %+v, %v; want rolled_back", out, err)
			}
			calls := f.callsSince(mark)
			if urCalled(calls, "StartContainer") != tc.wantStart {
				t.Errorf("calls %v: StartContainer called = %v, want %v", calls, !tc.wantStart, tc.wantStart)
			}
			for _, destructive := range []string{"RemoveContainer", "CreateContainer", "RemoveVolume", "CreateVolume", "CopyToContainer"} {
				if urCalled(calls, destructive) {
					t.Errorf("calls %v include %s, want the original left in place", calls, destructive)
				}
			}
			if c, ok := f.h.docker.container(f.inst.ContainerID); !ok || !c.State.Running {
				t.Errorf("the original container %+v (found %v), want it running", c, ok)
			}
			if got := f.phase(); got != "rolled_back" {
				t.Errorf("phase %q, want rolled_back", got)
			}
			_, dataKept := f.h.managed[backupDir+"/"+urChange+"-data.tar"]
			_, certKept := f.h.managed[backupDir+"/"+urChange+"-letsencrypt.tar"]
			if tc.wantKeeps && (!dataKept || !certKept) {
				t.Errorf("archives kept: data %v, letsencrypt %v, want both", dataKept, certKept)
			}
			if tc.wantNoFile && (dataKept || certKept) {
				t.Errorf("archives left: data %v, letsencrypt %v, want none", dataKept, certKept)
			}
			mark = f.mark()
			again, err := f.recover()
			if err != nil || again.State != core.RecoveryRolledBack || len(f.callsSince(mark)) != 0 {
				t.Errorf("second recovery %+v, %v after Docker calls %v; want rolled_back again without touching Docker", again, err, f.callsSince(mark))
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestUrRecoverWithoutARecord: a crash before the transaction was recorded changed nothing on the host.
func TestUrRecoverWithoutARecord(t *testing.T) {
	f := urSetup(t)
	mark := f.mark()
	out, err := f.recover()
	if err != nil || out.State != core.RecoveryRolledBack || len(f.callsSince(mark)) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("outcome %+v, %v after Docker calls %v; want rolled_back without touching Docker", out, err, f.callsSince(mark))
	}
}

// TestUrRecoverTerminalPhases: a finished transaction is reported as it ended and the host is not touched.
func TestUrRecoverTerminalPhases(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name, phase, result string
		want                string
	}{
		{"done", "done", "", core.RecoveryCompleted},
		{"rolled back", "rolled_back", "", core.RecoveryRolledBack},
		{"failed", "failed", "the API did not answer", core.RecoveryNeedsRepair},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			f := urSetup(t)
			f.record(tc.phase, tc.result, nil)
			mark := f.mark()
			out, err := f.recover()
			if err != nil || out.State != tc.want || len(f.callsSince(mark)) != 0 {
				t.Fatalf("outcome %+v, %v after Docker calls %v; want %s without touching Docker", out, err, f.callsSince(mark), tc.want)
			}
			switch tc.phase {
			case "done":
				var got Upgraded
				if err := json.Unmarshal(out.Output, &got); err != nil || got != (Upgraded{FromImage: urFrom, ToImage: urTo}) {
					t.Errorf("output %s (%v), want the upgrade from %s to %s", out.Output, err, urFrom, urTo)
				}
			case "failed":
				if !strings.Contains(out.Reason, urTo) || !strings.Contains(out.Reason, tc.result) {
					t.Errorf("reason %q, want the target image and the transaction's result", out.Reason)
				}
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestUrRecoverAfterRecreation: from recreating on, the host is brought back to the original by the rollback,
// whether or not the replacement container exists yet, and also when the replacement runs the same image.
func TestUrRecoverAfterRecreation(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name  string
		phase string
		newTo string // the image of the replacement container; empty: none was created yet
	}{
		{"recreating, the replacement exists", "recreating", urTo},
		{"recreating, the original was removed and nothing replaced it", "recreating", ""},
		{"waiting", "waiting", urTo},
		{"waiting, the replacement runs the same image", "waiting", urFrom},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			f := urSetup(t)
			f.replace(tc.newTo)
			f.record(tc.phase, "", f.archives())
			mark := f.mark()
			out, err := f.recover()
			if err != nil || out.State != core.RecoveryRolledBack {
				t.Fatalf("outcome %+v, %v; want rolled_back", out, err)
			}
			if !urCalled(f.callsSince(mark), "CopyToContainer") {
				t.Errorf("calls %v, want the archives copied back", f.callsSince(mark))
			}
			f.restored()
		})
	}
}

// TestUrRecoverWhenTheRollbackFails: a rollback that cannot finish leaves the transaction open for the operator.
func TestUrRecoverWhenTheRollbackFails(t *testing.T) {
	f := urSetup(t)
	f.replace(urTo)
	f.record("waiting", "", f.archives())
	f.h.docker.Fail("CreateContainer", errors.New("create refused"))
	out, err := f.recover()
	if err != nil || out.State != core.RecoveryNeedsRepair || !strings.Contains(out.Reason, urTo) {
		t.Fatalf("outcome %+v, %v; want needs_repair naming %s", out, err, urTo)
	}
	if got := f.phase(); got == "rolled_back" {
		t.Errorf("phase %q, want a phase that is not rolled_back", got)
	}
}

// TestUrRecoverStartFailures: a Docker error while starting the original is returned, so that the engine retries
// the recovery; an original that is gone needs the operator.
func TestUrRecoverStartFailures(t *testing.T) {
	t.Run("the start fails", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := urSetup(t)
		f.setRunning(f.inst.ContainerID, false)
		f.record("stopped", "", nil)
		f.h.docker.Fail("StartContainer", errors.New("start refused"))
		if _, err := f.recover(); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "start refused") {
			t.Errorf("error %v, want the start's error", err)
		}
		if got := f.phase(); got != "stopped" {
			t.Errorf("phase %q, want stopped: the original did not run again", got)
		}
	})
	t.Run("the original is gone", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := urSetup(t)
		f.replace("")
		f.record("archived", "", f.archives())
		out, err := f.recover()
		if err != nil || out.State != core.RecoveryNeedsRepair || !strings.Contains(out.Reason, containerName) {
			t.Errorf("outcome %+v, %v; want needs_repair naming %s", out, err, containerName)
		}
	})
}

// TestUrInverse: undoing a finished upgrade restores the original, undoing a rolled-back one does nothing, and
// anything else is refused before the host is touched.
func TestUrInverse(t *testing.T) {
	t.Run("done restores the original", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := urSetup(t)
		f.replace(urTo)
		f.record("done", "", f.archives())
		if err := f.inverse(urResource); err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		f.restored()
	})
	t.Run("rolled back is already undone", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := urSetup(t)
		f.record("rolled_back", "", nil)
		mark := f.mark()
		if err := f.inverse(urResource); err != nil || len(f.callsSince(mark)) != 0 {
			t.Errorf("error %v after Docker calls %v, want none of either", err, f.callsSince(mark))
		}
	})
	for _, phase := range []string{"recorded", "archived", "waiting", "failed"} {
		t.Run(phase+" has nothing to undo", func(t *testing.T) {
			f := urSetup(t)
			f.record(phase, "", f.archives())
			mark := f.mark()
			err := f.inverse(urResource)
			if err == nil || !core.IsDefinite(err) || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), phase) || len(f.callsSince(mark)) != 0 {
				t.Errorf("error %v (definite %v) after Docker calls %v, want a definite refusal naming %q", err, core.IsDefinite(err), f.callsSince(mark), phase)
			}
		})
	}
	t.Run("no transaction", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := urSetup(t)
		if err := f.inverse(urResource); err == nil || !core.IsDefinite(err) {
			t.Errorf("error %v, want a definite refusal", err)
		}
	})
	t.Run("another resource", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := urSetup(t)
		f.record("done", "", f.archives())
		if err := f.inverse("npm.container:abc"); err == nil || !core.IsDefinite(err) {
			t.Errorf("error %v, want a definite refusal", err)
		}
	})
	t.Run("an archive was pruned", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := urSetup(t)
		f.replace(urTo)
		refs := f.archives()
		delete(f.h.files, backupDir+"/"+refs[1].File)
		f.record("done", "", refs)
		mark := f.mark()
		err := f.inverse(urResource)
		if err == nil || !core.IsDefinite(err) || errcode.From(err).Code != errcode.NotFound || len(f.callsSince(mark)) != 0 {
			t.Errorf("error %v after Docker calls %v, want a definite %s refusal before the host is touched", err, f.callsSince(mark), errcode.NotFound)
		}
		if c, ok := f.h.docker.container(containerName); !ok || c.ID != urNewID {
			t.Errorf("container %+v (found %v), want the upgraded one left in place", c, ok)
		}
	})
	t.Run("an archive does not match its checksum", func(t *testing.T) {
		f := urSetup(t)
		f.replace(urTo)
		refs := f.archives()
		f.h.files[backupDir+"/"+refs[0].File] = []byte("tampered")
		f.record("done", "", refs)
		mark := f.mark()
		err := f.inverse(urResource)
		if err == nil || !core.IsDefinite(err) || len(f.callsSince(mark)) != 0 {
			t.Errorf("error %v after Docker calls %v, want a definite refusal before the host is touched", err, f.callsSince(mark))
		}
	})
}
```

Two fixture details come from Task 11 and Task 22 and are the only places this test touches their code: `urFixture.setImage` and `replace` set both `ContainerJSON.Image` and `Config.Image`, because the upgrade's `Observe` may read either; and `urFixture.archives` records `archiveRef.Volume` as the Docker volume name (`darkpanel-npm-data`, `darkpanel-npm-letsencrypt`) and `File` as `<change id>-data.tar` and `<change id>-letsencrypt.tar`. If `instance_upgrade.go` records `Volume` in another form, change `archives` to that form; nothing else in the test depends on it.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests to verify they fail**

Run: `mkdir -p /root/.cache/w6a-tmp/24 && export TMPDIR=/root/.cache/w6a-tmp/24 GOTOOLCHAIN=local && timeout 300 go test -count=1 -run 'TestUr' ./internal/modules/npm/`
Expected: the package builds and every `TestUr` test FAILs with `handler has Inverse false and Recover false; want both`, because `instanceUpgradeHandler` has neither method yet.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

In `upgrade_recover.go`, on `instanceUpgradeHandler` (Task 22's type, in `instance_upgrade.go`, which this task does not edit):

1. `Inverse(ctx, env, e)` undoes the upgrade of change `e.ChangeID`. Every refusal before the host is touched is wrapped in `core.Definite`; no Docker call is made by a refusal.
   1. `e.Resource` other than `npm.instance:image`: refuse, error string `npm: "<resource>" is not a resource npm.instance.upgrade changes`.
   2. `loadUpgrade(ctx, env.DB.Read(), e.ChangeID)`: a read error is refused as is; no row is refused with `npm: no upgrade is recorded for change <id>`.
   3. Phase `rolled_back`: return `core.InverseResult{}` and a nil error, touching nothing.
   4. Phase `done`: first check every archive of the row: read `backupDir + "/" + a.File` through `h.n.d.readFile`; a file for which `errors.Is(err, fs.ErrNotExist)` holds is refused with `errcode.New(errcode.NotFound, "the backup archive <file> of the upgrade no longer exists")`, any other read error is refused as is, and a SHA-256 (hex) that differs from `a.SHA256` is refused with `npm: archive <file> does not match its recorded checksum`. Only when all pass, call `h.rollback(ctx, env, u)` and return its error unchanged (it is not definite: the host may have changed) or `core.InverseResult{}`. The pre-check exists so that an undo can never destroy the upgraded container and then find no archive to restore.
   5. Any other phase: refuse with `npm: the upgrade of change <id> is in phase "<phase>", which has nothing to undo`.
2. `Recover(ctx, env)` decides by the row of `env.ChangeID` alone and journals nothing (the engine settles the effect through the upgrade's `Observe` afterwards). It returns an error only for a failed row read, an unexpected Docker failure while inspecting or starting the original, or a failed phase write; the engine then retries it.
   - no row: nothing was recorded, so nothing on the host changed: `core.RecoveryRolledBack`, no Docker call.
   - `rolled_back`: `core.RecoveryRolledBack`, no Docker call, so a second run changes nothing.
   - `done`: `core.RecoveryCompleted` with `Output` the JSON of `Upgraded{FromImage: u.FromImage, ToImage: u.ToImage}`, no Docker call.
   - `failed`: `core.RecoveryNeedsRepair` with `Reason` "the upgrade of Nginx Proxy Manager to <ToImage> failed: <Result>", no Docker call. A failed row means the rollback itself failed, and it is not repeated without the operator.
   - `recreating` and `waiting`: call `h.rollback(ctx, env, u)` unconditionally, as spec 6's table says, so a replacement that runs the same image as the original is replaced too. If it fails, return `core.RecoveryNeedsRepair` with `Reason` "the rollback of the upgrade to <ToImage> failed: <error text>" and a nil error. On success return `core.RecoveryRolledBack`; `rollback` has set the phase.
   - `recorded`, `stopped`, `archiving`, `archived`: the original container was never removed. For `archiving` first remove the archives of this change that may be half written: `env.Files.RemoveManaged(ctx, ID, backupDir, name)` for the two names `archiveName` gives for the data and the letsencrypt volume (read how `instance_upgrade.go` calls `archiveName` and use the same form; `RemoveManaged` of a missing file is not an error). Verified archives of `archived` stay. Then inspect `containerName` through `h.n.d.docker`: when `dockerapi.IsNotFound` holds, return `core.RecoveryNeedsRepair` with `Reason` "the original container darkpanel-npm is missing" and a nil error; when it is not running, start it with `StartContainer`; then `setUpgradePhase(ctx, env.DB, env.ChangeID, "rolled_back", "", u.Archives, env.Clock.Now())` and return `core.RecoveryRolledBack`. A running original is not started again.
   `Recover` writes `rolled_back` only after the original runs again, so a failed start leaves the row as it was.
3. Error strings start with `npm:`. Neither method returns, logs or journals a credential: the upgrade row holds none.

```go
package npm

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"reflect"
	"regexp"
	"slices"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
	"testing"

	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/schema"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/modules/firewall"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/modules/firewall/dockerfw"
)

// cxHost is the NPM host of these tests; the test host's own id ("h1") is deliberately another.
const cxHost = "h7"

// cxSteps asks def for its steps the way the engine does, through the erased handler.
func cxSteps(t *testing.T, def core.ActionDef, in any) ([]core.Step, error) {
	t.Helper()
	raw, err := json.Marshal(in)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return def.Handler.Steps(context.Background(), core.Env{}, raw)
}

// cxGeneric is v as decoded JSON.
func cxGeneric(t *testing.T, v any) any {
	t.Helper()
	raw, err := json.Marshal(v)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var out any
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &out); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return out
}

var cxRef = regexp.MustCompile(`\$\{([^}]*)\}`)

// cxResolve resolves the references of a step template against the composite's input in and the outputs of earlier
// steps, as the engine does: a string that is exactly one reference becomes the referenced value with its JSON type,
// a reference inside a longer string is replaced by the value's text, and a value that was substituted is never read
// as a template again. A reference to a member that does not exist fails the test, as it fails the plan.
func cxResolve(t *testing.T, tmpl json.RawMessage, in any, outputs map[string]any) json.RawMessage {
	t.Helper()
	input := cxGeneric(t, in)
	steps := map[string]any{}
	for id, v := range outputs {
		steps[id] = map[string]any{"output": cxGeneric(t, v)}
	}
	lookup := func(ref string) any {
		parts := strings.Split(ref, ".")
		var cur any
		switch parts[0] {
		case "input":
			cur = input
		case "steps":
			cur = steps
		default:
			t.Errorf("reference ${%s} is neither an input nor a step reference", ref)
			return nil
		}
		for _, p := range parts[1:] {
			switch c := cur.(type) {
			case map[string]any:
				next, ok := c[p]
				if !ok {
					t.Errorf("reference ${%s}: there is no member %q", ref, p)
					return nil
				}
				cur = next
			case []any:
				i, err := strconv.Atoi(p)
				if err != nil || i < 0 || i >= len(c) {
					t.Errorf("reference ${%s}: %q is not an index of the array", ref, p)
					return nil
				}
				cur = c[i]
			default:
				t.Errorf("reference ${%s}: %q is looked up in a value that has no members", ref, p)
				return nil
			}
		}
		return cur
	}
	var walk func(v any) any
	walk = func(v any) any {
		switch x := v.(type) {
		case string:
			if m := cxRef.FindStringSubmatch(x); m != nil && m[0] == x {
				return lookup(m[1])
			}
			return cxRef.ReplaceAllStringFunc(x, func(s string) string { return fmt.Sprint(lookup(s[2 : len(s)-1])) })
		case map[string]any:
			for k, e := range x {
				x[k] = walk(e)
			}
		case []any:
			for i, e := range x {
				x[i] = walk(e)
			}
		}
		return v
	}
	var tv any
	if err := json.Unmarshal(tmpl, &tv); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("template %s: %v", tmpl, err)
	}
	out, err := json.Marshal(walk(tv))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return out
}

// cxStrict decodes raw into a T and refuses a member T does not have.
func cxStrict[T any](t *testing.T, raw json.RawMessage) T {
	t.Helper()
	var v T
	dec := json.NewDecoder(bytes.NewReader(raw))
	dec.DisallowUnknownFields()
	if err := dec.Decode(&v); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("input %s: %v", raw, err)
	}
	return v
}

// cxWantStep fails the test unless s is the step id on host running action after the steps after.
func cxWantStep(t *testing.T, s core.Step, id, host, action string, after []string) {
	t.Helper()
	if s.ID != id || s.Host != host || s.Action.String() != action || !slices.Equal(s.After, after) {
		t.Errorf("step %+v, want %s: %s on host %q after %v", s, id, action, host, after)
	}
}

// cxIDs are the ids of steps, in order.
func cxIDs(steps []core.Step) []string {
	var ids []string
	for _, s := range steps {
		ids = append(ids, s.ID)
	}
	return ids
}

// cxRefs counts the step references in the template of s.
func cxRefs(s core.Step) int { return strings.Count(string(s.Input), "${steps.") }

// cxAction returns the declaration of action name, version 1, of module m.
func cxAction(t *testing.T, m core.Module, name string) core.ActionDef {
	t.Helper()
	for _, a := range m.Manifest().Actions {
		if a.Spec.Name == name && a.Spec.Version == 1 {
			return a
		}
	}
	t.Fatalf("%s is not declared", name)
	return core.ActionDef{}
}

// cxJSON is v as the JSON a step output holds.
func cxJSON(t *testing.T, v any) json.RawMessage {
	t.Helper()
	raw, err := json.Marshal(v)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return raw
}

// TestCxDeclarations: the three are controller-side composites that take the module's coordination lock; the
// exposure and the deployment unwind in reverse, the removal forward.
func TestCxDeclarations(t *testing.T) {
	n := newTHHost(t).nm()
	tests := []struct {
		name   string
		def    core.ActionDef
		unwind core.Unwind
	}{
		{"npm.instance.deploy-public", n.instanceDeployPublic(), core.UnwindReverse},
		{"npm.expose.container", n.exposeContainer(), core.UnwindReverse},
		{"npm.expose.remove", n.exposeRemove(), core.UnwindForward},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		s := tc.def.Spec
		if s.Name != tc.name || s.Version != 1 || s.Summary == "" || s.Class != core.ClassComposite || s.Side != core.SideController ||
			s.Unwind != tc.unwind || !slices.Equal(s.LockKeys, []string{"composite:npm"}) {
			t.Errorf("%s: spec %+v, want version 1, a summary, a controller-side composite, unwind %v, lock composite:npm", tc.name, s, tc.unwind)
		}
		if tc.def.Handler.Steps == nil || tc.def.Handler.Output == nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: the handler lacks Steps or Output", tc.name)
		}
		if _, err := schema.ActionSnapshot(tc.def); err != nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: schema: %v", tc.name, err)
		}
	}
}

// TestCxDeployPublicSteps: the deployment checks the host first; the ports step exists only with the integration,
// is the last step, and is a valid input of the revertible firewall action.
func TestCxDeployPublicSteps(t *testing.T) {
	for _, integration := range []bool{false, true} {
		t.Run(fmt.Sprintf("integration %v", integration), func(t *testing.T) {
			in := DeployPublicInput{HostID: cxHost, AcmeEmail: "ops@example.com", Integration: integration}
			steps, err := cxSteps(t, newTHHost(t).nm().instanceDeployPublic(), in)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatal(err)
			}
			wantIDs := []string{"check", "deploy"}
			if integration {
				wantIDs = append(wantIDs, "ports")
			}
			if !slices.Equal(cxIDs(steps), wantIDs) {
				t.Fatalf("steps %v, want %v", cxIDs(steps), wantIDs)
			}
			cxWantStep(t, steps[0], "check", "", "npm.instance.check/v1", nil)
			if got := cxStrict[CheckInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[0].Input, in, nil)); got != (CheckInput{HostID: cxHost, Integration: integration}) {
				t.Errorf("check input %+v, want host %s and integration %v", got, cxHost, integration)
			}
			cxWantStep(t, steps[1], "deploy", cxHost, "npm.instance.deploy/v1", []string{"check"})
			if got := cxStrict[DeployInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[1].Input, in, nil)); got != (DeployInput{AcmeEmail: "ops@example.com"}) {
				t.Errorf("deploy input %+v, want the ACME email", got)
			}
			if !integration {
				return
			}
			cxWantStep(t, steps[2], "ports", cxHost, "firewall.containers.apply/v1", []string{"deploy"})
			ports := cxStrict[struct {
				Ops []firewall.ContainerOp `json:"ops"`
			}](t, steps[2].Input)
			want := []firewall.ContainerOp{
				{Op: "allow", Binding: &dockerfw.Binding{Selector: "darkpanel-npm", HostPort: 80, Proto: "tcp"}},
				{Op: "allow", Binding: &dockerfw.Binding{Selector: "darkpanel-npm", HostPort: 443, Proto: "tcp"}},
			}
			if !reflect.DeepEqual(ports.Ops, want) {
				t.Errorf("ports ops %+v, want allow of 80/tcp and 443/tcp of darkpanel-npm from every address and source", ports.Ops)
			}
			def := cxAction(t, firewall.Module(), "firewall.containers.apply")
			if def.Spec.Class != core.ClassRevertible || def.Spec.Side != core.SideAgent {
				t.Errorf("firewall.containers.apply is %s on the %s side, want a revertible agent action: the one step that may be, last", def.Spec.Class, def.Spec.Side)
			}
			if err := schema.NewValidator().ValidateInput(def, steps[2].Input); err != nil {
				t.Errorf("ports input %s is invalid for firewall.containers.apply: %v", steps[2].Input, err)
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestCxDeployPublicKeepsInputOutOfTheTemplate: the engine reads every "${" of a template as a reference, so what the
// operator typed reaches the steps only through references to the input.
func TestCxDeployPublicKeepsInputOutOfTheTemplate(t *testing.T) {
	in := DeployPublicInput{HostID: "${steps.deploy.output.containerId}", AcmeEmail: "${steps.check.output.integration}@example.com", Integration: true}
	steps, err := cxSteps(t, newTHHost(t).nm().instanceDeployPublic(), in)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	for _, s := range steps {
		if cxRefs(s) != 0 {
			t.Errorf("step %s template %s holds a step reference, want references to the input only", s.ID, s.Input)
		}
	}
	if got := cxStrict[CheckInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[0].Input, in, nil)); got.HostID != in.HostID {
		t.Errorf("check host %q, want %q", got.HostID, in.HostID)
	}
	if got := cxStrict[DeployInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[1].Input, in, nil)); got.AcmeEmail != in.AcmeEmail {
		t.Errorf("deploy email %q, want %q", got.AcmeEmail, in.AcmeEmail)
	}
}

func TestCxDeployPublicOutput(t *testing.T) {
	def := newTHHost(t).nm().instanceDeployPublic()
	deployed := cxJSON(t, Deployed{ContainerID: "abc123", Image: "jc21/nginx-proxy-manager:2.13.5", AdminEmail: "ops@example.com"})
	tests := []struct {
		name        string
		integration bool
		steps       map[string]json.RawMessage
		want        DeployPublicOutput
	}{
		{"without the integration", false, map[string]json.RawMessage{"deploy": deployed}, DeployPublicOutput{ContainerID: "abc123"}},
		{"with the integration", true, map[string]json.RawMessage{"deploy": deployed, "ports": json.RawMessage(`{}`)}, DeployPublicOutput{ContainerID: "abc123", PortsOpened: true}},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			in := cxJSON(t, DeployPublicInput{HostID: cxHost, AcmeEmail: "ops@example.com", Integration: tc.integration})
			out, err := def.Handler.Output(context.Background(), core.Env{}, in, tc.steps)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatal(err)
			}
			if got := cxStrict[DeployPublicOutput](t, out); got != tc.want {
				t.Errorf("output %+v, want %+v", got, tc.want)
			}
		})
	}
	in := cxJSON(t, DeployPublicInput{HostID: cxHost, AcmeEmail: "ops@example.com"})
	if _, err := def.Handler.Output(context.Background(), core.Env{}, in, map[string]json.RawMessage{}); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "deploy") {
		t.Errorf("output without the deploy step: %v, want an error naming deploy", err)
	}
}

// TestCxExposeContainerSteps: a public instance is required first, the proxy is attached to the target's network
// and holds a certificate before the proxy host is added, and the proxy host forwards to the container by name.
func TestCxExposeContainerSteps(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {
		name string
		in   ExposeInput
		cert int // the certificate id the cert step answers
		want ProxyHostInput
	}{
		{"every option", ExposeInput{HostID: cxHost, Container: "web", Port: 8080, Network: "appnet", Domains: []string{"app.example.com", "www.example.com"}, Websockets: true, AccessListID: 3}, 42,
			ProxyHostInput{Domains: []string{"app.example.com", "www.example.com"}, ForwardScheme: "http", ForwardHost: "web", ForwardPort: 8080, CertificateID: 42, AccessListID: 3, SSLForced: true, HSTS: true, Websockets: true}},
		{"no options", ExposeInput{HostID: cxHost, Container: "api", Port: 80, Network: "backend", Domains: []string{"api.example.com"}}, 7,
			ProxyHostInput{Domains: []string{"api.example.com"}, ForwardScheme: "http", ForwardHost: "api", ForwardPort: 80, CertificateID: 7, SSLForced: true, HSTS: true}},
	}
	for _, tc := range tests {
		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			steps, err := cxSteps(t, newTHHost(t).nm().exposeContainer(), tc.in)
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatal(err)
			}
			if want := []string{"public", "network", "cert", "proxy"}; !slices.Equal(cxIDs(steps), want) {
				t.Fatalf("steps %v, want %v", cxIDs(steps), want)
			}
			cxWantStep(t, steps[0], "public", "", "npm.public.check/v1", nil)
			if got := cxStrict[PublicInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[0].Input, tc.in, nil)); got != (PublicInput{HostID: cxHost}) {
				t.Errorf("public input %+v, want host %s", got, cxHost)
			}
			cxWantStep(t, steps[1], "network", cxHost, "npm.network.attach/v1", []string{"public"})
			wantAttach := AttachInput{Network: tc.in.Network, Claimant: "expose:" + tc.in.Domains[0], Target: tc.in.Container}
			if got := cxStrict[AttachInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[1].Input, tc.in, nil)); got != wantAttach {
				t.Errorf("attach input %+v, want %+v: the claimant names the first domain and the target is the container", got, wantAttach)
			}
			cxWantStep(t, steps[2], "cert", cxHost, "npm.certificate.request/v1", []string{"public"})
			if got := cxStrict[CertificateInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[2].Input, tc.in, nil)); !reflect.DeepEqual(got, CertificateInput{Domains: tc.in.Domains}) {
				t.Errorf("certificate input %+v, want every domain", got)
			}
			cxWantStep(t, steps[3], "proxy", cxHost, "npm.proxyhost.add/v1", []string{"network", "cert"})
			outputs := map[string]any{"cert": CertificateView{ID: tc.cert}}
			if got := cxStrict[ProxyHostInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[3].Input, tc.in, outputs)); !reflect.DeepEqual(got, tc.want) {
				t.Errorf("proxy input %+v, want %+v", got, tc.want)
			}
			if cxRefs(steps[3]) != 1 || cxRefs(steps[0])+cxRefs(steps[1])+cxRefs(steps[2]) != 0 {
				t.Errorf("step references: public %d, network %d, cert %d, proxy %d; want only the proxy step's reference to the certificate",
					cxRefs(steps[0]), cxRefs(steps[1]), cxRefs(steps[2]), cxRefs(steps[3]))
			}
		})
	}
}

// TestCxExposeContainerKeepsInputOutOfTheTemplate: container, network and domain names are typed by the operator and
// reach the steps only through references to the input.
func TestCxExposeContainerKeepsInputOutOfTheTemplate(t *testing.T) {
	in := ExposeInput{
		HostID: cxHost, Container: "${steps.cert.output.id}", Port: 80, Network: "${steps.network.output.attached}",
		Domains: []string{"${input.port}.example.com", "b.example.com"},
	}
	steps, err := cxSteps(t, newTHHost(t).nm().exposeContainer(), in)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	outputs := map[string]any{"cert": CertificateView{ID: 5}}
	attach := cxStrict[AttachInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[1].Input, in, outputs))
	if attach.Network != in.Network || attach.Target != in.Container || attach.Claimant != "expose:"+in.Domains[0] {
		t.Errorf("attach input %+v, want the typed values unchanged", attach)
	}
	proxy := cxStrict[ProxyHostInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[3].Input, in, outputs))
	if proxy.ForwardHost != in.Container || !reflect.DeepEqual(proxy.Domains, in.Domains) || proxy.CertificateID != 5 {
		t.Errorf("proxy input %+v, want the typed values unchanged and the certificate 5", proxy)
	}
}

func TestCxExposeContainerOutput(t *testing.T) {
	def := newTHHost(t).nm().exposeContainer()
	in := cxJSON(t, ExposeInput{HostID: cxHost, Container: "web", Port: 80, Network: "appnet", Domains: []string{"app.example.com"}})
	steps := map[string]json.RawMessage{
		"network": cxJSON(t, Attachment{Network: "appnet", Claimant: "expose:app.example.com", Attached: true}),
		"cert":    cxJSON(t, CertificateView{ID: 7}),
		"proxy":   cxJSON(t, ProxyHostView{ID: 11}),
	}
	out, err := def.Handler.Output(context.Background(), core.Env{}, in, steps)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if got := cxStrict[ExposeOutput](t, out); got != (ExposeOutput{ProxyHostID: 11, CertificateID: 7}) {
		t.Errorf("output %+v, want proxy host 11 and certificate 7", got)
	}
	for _, missing := range []string{"proxy", "cert"} {
		partial := map[string]json.RawMessage{}
		for k, v := range steps {
			if k != missing {
				partial[k] = v
			}
		}
		if _, err := def.Handler.Output(context.Background(), core.Env{}, in, partial); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), missing) {
			t.Errorf("output without the %s step: %v, want an error naming it", missing, err)
		}
	}
}

// TestCxExposeRemoveSteps: the proxy host goes first, then every claim of the exposure on the proxy's networks.
func TestCxExposeRemoveSteps(t *testing.T) {
	in := ExposeRemoveInput{HostID: cxHost, Domain: "app.example.com"}
	steps, err := cxSteps(t, newTHHost(t).nm().exposeRemove(), in)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if want := []string{"proxy", "network"}; !slices.Equal(cxIDs(steps), want) {
		t.Fatalf("steps %v, want %v", cxIDs(steps), want)
	}
	cxWantStep(t, steps[0], "proxy", cxHost, "npm.proxyhost.remove/v1", nil)
	if got := cxStrict[ProxyHostRemoveInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[0].Input, in, nil)); got != (ProxyHostRemoveInput{Domain: "app.example.com"}) {
		t.Errorf("remove input %+v, want the domain and no id", got)
	}
	cxWantStep(t, steps[1], "network", cxHost, "npm.network.detach/v1", []string{"proxy"})
	if got := cxStrict[DetachInput](t, cxResolve(t, steps[1].Input, in, nil)); got != (DetachInput{Network: "", Claimant: "expose:app.example.com"}) {
		t.Errorf("detach input %+v, want every claim of expose:app.example.com", got)
	}
	if cxRefs(steps[0])+cxRefs(steps[1]) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("the templates %s and %s hold step references, want references to the input only", steps[0].Input, steps[1].Input)
	}
}

func TestCxExposeRemoveOutput(t *testing.T) {
	def := newTHHost(t).nm().exposeRemove()
	for _, domain := range []string{"app.example.com", "www.example.com"} {
		in := cxJSON(t, ExposeRemoveInput{HostID: cxHost, Domain: domain})
		steps := map[string]json.RawMessage{"proxy": cxJSON(t, ProxyHostView{ID: 11}), "network": cxJSON(t, struct{}{})}
		out, err := def.Handler.Output(context.Background(), core.Env{}, in, steps)
		if err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		if got := cxStrict[ExposeRemoveOutput](t, out); got != (ExposeRemoveOutput{Domain: domain}) {
			t.Errorf("output %+v, want domain %s", got, domain)
		}
	}
	in := cxJSON(t, ExposeRemoveInput{HostID: cxHost, Domain: "app.example.com"})
	if _, err := def.Handler.Output(context.Background(), core.Env{}, in, map[string]json.RawMessage{"proxy": cxJSON(t, ProxyHostView{ID: 11})}); err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "network") {
		t.Errorf("output without the network step: %v, want an error naming network", err)
	}
}
```

The tests decode every step input strictly into the wire type of the action it feeds, so the member names a template uses must be the `json` names of Task 11's `wire.go`; Contracts prescribes lowerCamelCase, and `wire.go` is the authority if a name differs.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

In `composite_deploy_expose.go`, each constructor is `core.DefineComposite[In, Out]` with `Version: 1`, a one-sentence summary and the `Unwind` named above (`DefineComposite` sets the class, the controller side and the lock `composite:npm`). Summaries: deploy-public "Deploy Nginx Proxy Manager and, when the host's Docker firewall integration is on, open ports 80 and 443 for it."; expose.container "Attach the proxy to a container's network, request a certificate and add a proxy host that forwards to the container."; expose.remove "Remove an exposed container's proxy host and its network claims." Each composer has `Steps` and `Output` methods.

Rules for every template (the engine reads each `${` of a template as a reference, so what an operator typed must never be spliced into a template): a string the operator typed (host id, e-mail, container, network, domains) is passed only as a whole-string reference to the input, such as `${input.acmeEmail}`, which keeps its JSON type, or inside a longer string as `expose:${input.domains.0}`; integers and booleans that cannot hold `${` (the port, the access list id, the flags, and the constants `forwardScheme` `http`, `sslForced` and `hsts` `true`) are written as literals. A step's `Host` is the composite's `HostID` for agent actions and empty for the two controller reads. Build each step input as a map marshalled with `encoding/json` by a helper `exposeStepInput(map[string]any) (json.RawMessage, error)` and use the member names of `wire.go`. Each composer is a struct type named `deployPublicComposer`, `exposeContainerComposer` and `exposeRemoveComposer`.

1. `npm.instance.deploy-public`:
   - `check` (`npm.instance.check`, no host, no `After`): `hostId` is `${input.hostId}` and `integration` is the literal `in.Integration`.
   - `deploy` (`npm.instance.deploy`, host `in.HostID`, `After: ["check"]`): `acmeEmail` is `${input.acmeEmail}`.
   - only when `in.Integration`: `ports` (`firewall.containers.apply`, host `in.HostID`, `After: ["deploy"]`, the last and only revertible step). Its input is a struct with one member `ops` (JSON `ops`) holding two `firewall.ContainerOp` values with `Op: "allow"` and `Binding: &dockerfw.Binding{Selector: containerName, HostPort: 80, Proto: "tcp"}`, then the same with port 443; no host address and no source, so the rule is open to every address and source. The input carries no `core.Guarded` member, as the derp composite's `firewall.rules.apply` input does not.
   - `Output` reads the `deploy` step's `Deployed` and returns `DeployPublicOutput{ContainerID: <its ContainerID>, PortsOpened: in.Integration}`; a missing `deploy` output is an error saying "npm: the deployment has no output of its deploy step".
2. `npm.expose.container` (steps in this order):
   - `public` (`npm.public.check`, no host, no `After`): `hostId` is `${input.hostId}`.
   - `network` (`npm.network.attach`, `After: ["public"]`): `network` is `${input.network}`, `claimant` is the constant `expose:` followed by `${input.domains.0}` (the constant is declared once as `exposeClaimantPrefix`), `target` is `${input.container}`; no `address`.
   - `cert` (`npm.certificate.request`, `After: ["public"]`): `domains` is `${input.domains}`, the whole array.
   - `proxy` (`npm.proxyhost.add`, `After: ["network", "cert"]`): `domains` is `${input.domains}`, `forwardScheme` is `http`, `forwardHost` is `${input.container}`, `forwardPort` is the literal `in.Port`, `certificateId` is the whole-string reference `${steps.cert.output.id}`, `accessListId` is the literal `in.AccessListID`, `sslForced` and `hsts` are `true`, `websockets` is the literal `in.Websockets`. This is the only template with a step reference.
   - `Output` reads the `id` of the `proxy` step and of the `cert` step (a helper `exposeStepID(steps, id) (int, error)` decodes a struct whose one member `ID int` carries the JSON name `id`, and fails with "npm: the exposure has no output of its <id> step" when the step is absent) and returns `ExposeOutput{ProxyHostID, CertificateID}`.
3. `npm.expose.remove`:
   - `proxy` (`npm.proxyhost.remove`, host `in.HostID`, no `After`): `domain` is `${input.domain}`.
   - `network` (`npm.network.detach`, `After: ["proxy"]`): `network` is the empty string, written explicitly, and `claimant` is `expose:` followed by `${input.domain}`, which detaches every claim of that claimant.
   - `Output` requires the `network` step's output (error "npm: the removal has no output of its network step" otherwise) and returns `ExposeRemoveOutput{Domain: in.Domain}`.
4. `Steps` reads only its input; it takes no database or agent read. Error strings start with `npm:`. No new error code.
